The wind being againft us, we lay here all the ift of Septem- 
ber; but in the morning of the ad, a favourable breeze fpringing 
up, we weighed anchor, and made fail. The adjoining coaft feems 
not to be in a high ftate of cultivation ; yet at times it affords

could not behold the Caftle without calling to mind that cele-

cliffs, thofe objects fo pleafing toa failor, after a long voyage, and 
which I ardently hope to have the fatisfaction of again beholding

the sth, when a very heavy gale of wind fprung up, and our veflel 
was tofled about at the mercy of the winds and waves. Every 
thing now was hurry and diforder ; and the creaking of the fhip’s 
timbers, the noife of the people on deck running backwards and

yards added to the general contfufion, 


During this fcene, I kept below in the fteerage, in a fitu-

given way to the firft impulfes of fear, I fhould have been more 
dead than alive ; but I had recollection enough to reftect, that our

This curious operation of cutting the under lip of the females, 
never takes place during their infancy, but from every obfervation 
I was able to make, {cems confined to a peculiar period of life. 
When the girls arrive to the age of fourteen or fifteen, the center 
of the under lip, in -he thick part near the mouth, is fimply perfo- 
rated, and a piece of copper wire introduced to prevent the aper- 
ture from clofing , the aperture afterwards is lengthened, from time 
to time, in a line parallel with the mouth, and the wooden orna- 
ments are enlarged in proportion, till they are frequently increafed 
to three, oreven four inches in length, and nearly as wide, but this 
generally happens, when the matron is advanced in years, and con- 
fequently the mufcles are relaxed ; fo that poflibly old age may 
obtain greater refpect than this very fingular ornament. 


The manners and difpofition of the people here, approach nearer 
to thofe in Cook’s River, and Prince William’s Sound, than our 
friends in Port Mulgrave; but this may, perhaps, in fome mea- 
fure, be accounted for from their enlarged fociety, and their con- 
ftant intercourfe with each other. 


Their traffic, and indeed all their concerns, fo far as fell under 
my obfervation, appear to be conducted with great order and regu-

hour in finging before the traffic commenced. The Chief of the 
tribe has always the entire management of all the trade belonging 
to his people, and takes infinite pains to difpofe of their furs ad- 
vantageoully,

One peculiar cuftom I took notice of here, which as yet we had

generally the cuitom. Soon after day-light the next morning, our

Canton is fituated on the Tigris, about four leagues from

{hipping returns next feafon.

Nor is this capital defect in their language likely to be reme-

Their method of writing is in columns, from the top to the

lings, and pence; and they always begin at the right-hand margin

Their arithmetic is decimal, every thing being reckoned by 
tens. They have not any idea of addition by figures, but are very 
expert in cafting up any fums, by the help of wooden balls, 
which run on fall fpindles in a kind of open box.

At five o'clock in the afternoon of the 24th, we unmoored, and

This kind (as do all that are properly of this genus) adheres 
to coral rocks, by a ligament that comes from the animal through 
the hole in the larger valve. 


The internal ftructure (peculiar to fhells of this genus) is very 
fingular, and confifts of two teftaceous rays, which commence 
near the hinge in the leffer valve, where they adhere; from whence,, 
leaving the fhell, they proceed to near the edge, then bend to- 
wards the other valve, and turn back to their commencement, 
where they unite: this internal part is very delicate, and breaks 
upon the fmalleft touch, but is thicker in the part neareft to the 
larger valve. The fhell takes its name from certain parts of the 
animal, which run in a branched form along the infide of the 
fhell, which being held to a ftrong light, or a candle, gives it a 
beautifully veined appearance. The outfide is {mooth, and of a 
pale brown colour.
